### Military instruction



Army Cadet Preparatory School

Military teaching and instruction can be defined as the army's main missions in peacetime.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)275-224][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENeves,_Oficial_da_Reserva_e_a_Segurança_Nacional_(1964)13-225] This system encompasses education, instruction, and research.[^cite_note-instituições-226] Privates' and corporals' activities correspond to elementary education, sergeants' to secondary, technical or higher education ([technologist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelor_of_Technology)) and officers' to higher education.[^cite_note-227] Practically all military organizations have some responsibility for training soldiers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEViana,_História_institucional_e_cultura_organizacional_da_tropa_aeroterrestre_(2020)306-228] Active-duty organizations have a fixed personnel, but annually train a variable number of reservists. There are organizations exclusively focused on reservists: the Reserve Officer Training Bodies (*Órgãos de Formação de Oficiais da Reserva*; OFOR), which consist of the Preparation Centers (*Centros de Preparação*; CPOR) and the Preparation Nuclei (*Núcleos de Preparação*; NPOR), and the *Tiros de Guerra*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)74-75-229] Other organizations train career military personnel (officers and sergeants).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEViana,_História_institucional_e_cultura_organizacional_da_tropa_aeroterrestre_(2020)306-228] By law, military education is regulated exclusively by the army itself.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_das_relações_pessoais_dos_militares_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2012)240-230]

Officers in the military education line, who carry out the army's core activity (warfare), enter the institution early, in the equivalent of the first year of higher education. After the [Army Cadet Preparatory School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escola_Preparat%C3%B3ria_de_Cadetes_do_Ex%C3%A9rcito) (*Escola Preparatória de Cadetes do Exército*; EsPCEx), in [Campinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campinas), they graduate from AMAN, in [Resende](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resende,_Rio_de_Janeiro).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos_&_Raposo,_A_elite_do_Exército_(2019)61-62-231] Officers' careers are organized into study cycles;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOliveira_&_Mathias,_Profissionalização_militar_(2020)51-232] AMAN trains [officer candidates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Officer_candidate),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos_&_Raposo,_A_elite_do_Exército_(2019)76-233] a degree from the Officer Improvement School (*Escola de Aperfeiçoamento de Oficiais*; EsAO) is necessary for promotion to major, and a degree from the [Army Command and General Staff School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escola_de_Comando_e_Estado-Maior_do_Ex%C3%A9rcito) (*Escola de Comando e Estado-Maior do Exército*; ECEME), to brigadier general.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos_&_Raposo,_A_elite_do_Exército_(2019)70-71-234] The AMAN degree is a bachelor's degree in Military Sciences, EsAO's degree is a master's degree in Military Operations, and ECEME's degree is a doctorate in Military Policy, Strategy and Administration; ECEME also offers a post-doctorate program.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOliveira_&_Mathias,_Profissionalização_militar_(2020)51-232]



Escola de Sargentos das Armas

Career combat sergeants graduate in two years, the first in one of the thirteen Army Technological School Units (*Unidades Escolares Tecnológicas do Exército*; UETE) spread across the country and the second in the [Combatant Sergeants' School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Army_Non-Commissioned_Officer_Academy) (*Escola de Sargentos das Armas*; ESA), Logistics Sergeants School (*Escola de Sargentos de Logística*; EsSLog) or Army Aviation Instruction Center (*Centro de Instrução de Aviação do Exército*; CIAvEx).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECosta,_A_nova_Escola_de_Formação_e_Graduação_de_Sargentos_de_Carreira_(2021)7-235] EsAO's equivalent is the Sergeants' Branches Improvement School (*Escola de Aperfeiçoamento de Sargentos das Armas*; EASA).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)72-73-236] The [Military Institute of Engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instituto_Militar_de_Engenharia) (*Instituto Militar de Engenharia*; IME), Army School of Health and Complementary Training (*Escola de Saúde e Formação Complementar do Exército*; EsFCEx) and the School of Specialized Instruction (*Escola de Instrução Especializada*; EsIE), among others, also participate in teaching sergeants and/or officers.[^cite_note-instituições-226][^cite_note-esfcex2-237]

The schools are complemented by specialization courses, extension courses and internships.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)73-238] In 2006, there were 66 specialization courses for officers and 76 for sergeants, eight extension courses for officers and six for sergeants, 47 internships for officers and 32 for sergeants.[^cite_note-239] This even covers education that is not strictly military, such as language courses offered by the Center for Personnel Studies.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_das_relações_pessoais_dos_militares_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2012)240-230] In the most difficult and coveted operational courses, such as those for commandos, jungle warriors and pathfinders, the rate of voluntary dismissal is high.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEViana,_História_institucional_e_cultura_organizacional_da_tropa_aeroterrestre_(2020)322-240]



Military Academy of Agulhas Negras

The Brazilian Army also maintains twelve Military Schools, at primary and secondary level, but their students do not necessarily pursue a military career.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOliveira_&_Mathias,_Profissionalização_militar_(2020)52-241] These schools offer the possibility of keeping officers' children in military education from a very early age.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos_&_Raposo,_A_elite_do_Exército_(2019)71-242] The Military Instruction Schools (*Escolas de Instrução Militar*; EsIM) are also secondary education, designed to allow students to carry out military service without interrupting their studies.[^cite_note-243]

According to the army, the training is rigorous, but ill-treatment, violence against lower ranks and bodily injuries are isolated cases and the instructions are controlled to avoid risks. Cases of violence by instructors exist; from 2005 to June 2015, the Rio de Janeiro Military Court judged 299 incidents in the army, navy and air force.[^cite_note-244] Critics point to the institution's corporatism as an obstacle to complaints. The aggressors are tried in the Military Court, but the compensation is paid by the federal government. An emblematic case was that of cadet Márcio Lapoente da Silveira, who died during training at AMAN in 1990, leading to a complaint to the [Inter-American Commission on Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-American_Commission_on_Human_Rights). The superior officer involved later reached the rank of colonel.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246]

### Branches, cadres and services



Anti-aircraft artillery radar



Quartermaster supply truck



River transposition with Engineering work

Troops are subdivided into several specialties, called "branches" (*armas*), "cadres" (*quadros*) and "services" (*serviços*), according to their missions and characteristics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESavian,_Resistências_à_mecanização_(2013)2-3-247] Seven of them, the military education line, directly carry out military operations, and their officers are exclusively trained at AMAN; they are the infantry, cavalry, engineering, artillery and communications branches, the Quartermaster Service (*Serviço de Intendência*) and the Ordnance Cadre (*Quadro de Material Bélico*). Colloquially, they are called "*armas*".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)42-248] Infantry and Cavalry are the so-called "base branches" (*armas base*), as they come into direct contact with the enemy. Artillery directly participates in combat, but from a distance, providing fire support. Engineering and communications are support branches, but they also act directly on the battlefield. Quartermasters and Ordnance are the theater of operations's logistics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)55-56-249]

The other areas of officers, with different origins, are the Military Engineers (*Quadro de Engenheiros Militares*; QEM), Auxiliary Officers (*Quadro Auxiliar de Oficiais*; QAO) and Complementary Officers (*Quadro Complementar de Oficiais*; QCO) and the Health and Religious Assistance Services.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFaria,_Armas,_Quadros_e_Serviços_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2022)101-104-250] Army Aviation, which is not a branch, has officers from different branches.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFerro,_Aviação_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2000)295-251] Warrant officers and sergeants belong to their own Military Qualifications (*Qualificações Militares de Sargentos*; QMS), covering, in addition to the seven areas of AMAN, Communications Maintenance, Topography, Aviation Maintenance, Aviation Support, Music and Health.[^cite_note-252][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERibeiro,_Proposta_de_integração_das_QMS_de_aviação_(2021)-253]

Officers and NCOs choose the branch in which they will serve during their training.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtassio,_Um_estudo_sociopolítico_sobre_a_formação_de_praças_(2012)34-254] This decision accompanies the soldier for the rest of his career and determines where he will serve and what commands he will be able to exercise.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECosta,_Tendências_ocupacionais_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2020)-255] However, more than a specialty, the branch is a social atmosphere of its own, determining the soldier's companions for the remainder of his career. Along with the class in which an officer graduated, the branch is the basic component of an officer's identity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)52-55-256] Temperament and disposition are presumed from branch collar insignia.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos_&_Raposo,_A_elite_do_Exército_(2019)66-219] Each branch has a "spirit", an association between the personal characteristics required in military operations and the individual's personality:[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECastro,_Um_antropólogo_na_caserna_(2004)70-71-257] "rustic" infantrymen, "fast" cavalrymen, "methodical" artillerymen, and so on. There are even negative stereotypes attributed to each branch by the others.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMello,_A_construção_da_identidade_militar_nas_alocuções_(2016)75-258]

The best students, based on the general classification, choose their branch first, and the last ones are "compelled" to fill the remaining vacancies.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtassio,_Um_estudo_sociopolítico_sobre_a_formação_de_praças_(2012)34-254] Until the 1990s, the students' preferred branches were infantry, cavalry and artillery, prestigious for being the combat ones, to which the most famous generals in the history of the army belonged. From then on, the Quartermaster and Ordnance, areas closest to civilian activity, became the most sought after by the new generations of cadets.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECosta,_Tendências_ocupacionais_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2020)-255][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)60-65-259]

### Officers

#### Subdivisions



Army chaplains

Career officers from the military education line, also called "AMAN officers" or "academy officers",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)29-30-260] are the backbone and elite of the institution,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos_&_Raposo,_A_elite_do_Exército_(2019)61-261] in which the army invests to represent it legitimately and, in the future, lead it.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)253-262] They are the majority of generals and all army generals, the top of the hierarchy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos_&_Raposo,_A_elite_do_Exército_(2019)64-263] But there are several other officer careers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_A_transformação_de_atitudes_e_valores_(2019)12-264]

2nd category reserve officers (R/2) are recruited through OFOR.[^cite_note-265] The Military Engineers Cadre (QEM) (not be confused with the military line Engineering Branch), is trained at the Military Engineering Institute and has skills closer to those of a university-trained engineer.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoreira,_O_discurso_nacionalista_dos_engenheiros_militares_(2008)3-266][^cite_note-267] A QEM engineer works in science, technology and the production of weapons.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFaria,_Armas,_Quadros_e_Serviços_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2022)101-268] The Auxiliary Officer Cadre (QAO) is made up of sergeants and warrant officers promoted to the officer ranks.[^cite_note-269] The Army's School of Health and Complementary Training trains the officers of the Complementary Cadre of Officers (QCO), Health Cadre (QSau) and Military Chaplains Cadre;[^cite_note-esfcex2-237] QCO officers already have a prior degree in a civil higher education course,[^cite_note-270] and military chaplains have prior training as Catholic or evangelical ministers.[^cite_note-271] Temporary Technical Officers (*Oficiais Técnicos Temporários*; OTT), also with prior academic training, are recruited after an internship and remain for up to eight years.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDias,_Elevação_nos_efetivos_de_Oficiais_Temporários_(2020)3-272]

#### Profile



AMAN officer candidate with his family at the graduation ceremony

The majority of officers come from middle-class families, often with military parents, in a pattern of endogenous recruitment that has existed since at least the 20th century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiranda,_A_construção_da_identidade_do_oficial_(2018)62-63-273] Increasingly, in the last decades of the 20th century, these military parents are junior officers and enlisted men seeking social advancement through lifelong public service. The children of senior officers often attend civilian universities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtassio,_Um_estudo_sociopolítico_sobre_a_formação_de_praças_(2012)65-66-274] Salaries have historically maintained officers in the middle class.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)164-275]

Young people do not enter AMAN thinking about leaving the military career in the future, or if they do, they are conditioned to change their minds. Officer status will follow each person until death.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos_&_Raposo,_A_elite_do_Exército_(2019)62-276] Permanent availability, exclusive dedication (i.e, the legal impossibility of assuming other positions), geographic mobility and restriction of labor rights are the characteristics of the career, according to the army.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)159-277] Geographic mobility means a nomadic career, with constant transfers. The result is a homogeneous army, but this brings difficulties for the officer's family and prevents rooting in local society.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)66-67-278] Due to exclusive dedication, the army advised against taking degrees outside the military, but this is changing in the 21st century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiranda,_A_construção_da_identidade_do_oficial_(2018)65-279]

An academy officer's contacts are established primarily within the institution.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)139-280] Officers and their families share a large part of their living, leisure and study environments, and the institution promotes internal social interaction, in which wives are fundamental.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECastro,_sobre_o_conceito_de_Instituição_Total_(2007)4-5-281] The family's presentation and behavior influence how an officer is qualified in professional life.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)107-282] The military family is also a bridge between the army and society as a whole.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)311-283] The notion of "Military Family" can be broad, encompassing military personnel and their families.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESilva,_Famílias_de_militares_(2013)863-284]

The profile of QCO officers is different, as they bring the worldview of academia and broader society into the army.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)141-142-285] R/2 officers have a different social origin: they are mainly university students, and the declared objective of the course is the "education of citizens, future members of the national elite and who have knowledge mainly of the values that are the bases of the Brazilian Army".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Razões_da_permanência_do_modelo_de_recrutamento_no_Brasil_(2001)85-86-286]

#### Career



General promotion ceremony

Out of a class of 400 AMAN officer candidates, only four become army generals. Rising through officer ranks takes decades and involves the principles of seniority, merit, choice, and in wartime, bravery. An example of a successful officer, [Fernando Azevedo e Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Azevedo_e_Silva) graduated from AMAN in 1976 and was promoted to army general in 2014.[^cite_note-promoções2-287] The political-military leaders of the 1960s graduated from the [Military School of Realengo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_School_of_Realengo) in the 1910s to 1930s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESvartman,_Generais_de_1964_(2006)30-288]

An officer's year of formation reveals his rank, his AMAN class (a group with great familiarity with each other and their own name, such as the "Independence Centennial" of 1989), and within it, his seniority.[^cite_note-289] There is a period of eight to ten years from AMAN to EsAO and three to ten years from there to ECEME.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOliveira_&_Mathias,_Profissionalização_militar_(2020)51-52-290] About a third of each class manages to enter ECEME,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)278-291] through which officers enter the Active Duty General Staff Cadre (*Quadro de Estado-Maior da Ativa*; QEMA). This opens up advisory and decision-making functions to them, and therefore, greater prestige and power. The other officers are part of the General Supplementary Cadre (*Quadro Suplementar Geral*; QSG), where they will have bureaucratic and/or executive functions until the end of their career. Only QEMA officers achieve generalship.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)70-74-292]

Once it's time for promotions to general, most of the class requests a transfer to the paid reserve. Only a minority is promoted to brigadier general, a political decision whose criterion is choice.[^cite_note-293] The decision involves the [Army High Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_High_Command) and the President of Brazil, who traditionally respects the names indicated by the High Command. Promoting a candidate junior to his peers usually pushes them to the reserve. An officer spends a maximum of twelve years as an active duty general.[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-promoções2-287][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHudson,_Brazil:_a_country_study_(1998)387-295]

### Warrant officers and sergeants



ESA's Artillery Course topography exercise

The motto "sergeant, the fundamental link between the command and the troops" summarizes the role assigned by the army to this rank.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZimmermann,_Sargentos_de_1964_(2013)16-296] The sergeant is both a leader and executor.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZimmermann,_Escolas_de_formação_e_aperfeiçoamento_dos_sargentos_(2022)448-297] In general, he is the one who maintains direct contact with the privates,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZimmermann,_Sargentos_de_1964_(2013)150-298] for whom he has the responsibility to discipline.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtassio,_Um_estudo_sociopolítico_sobre_a_formação_de_praças_(2012)214-299] His training aims to create a practical executor,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFerreira,_Uma_análise_dos_sistemas_simbólicos_da_Escola_de_Sargentos_das_Armas_(2022)371-300] who, if necessary, puts into practice what the officer thinks, but does not know how to execute.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtassio,_Um_estudo_sociopolítico_sobre_a_formação_de_praças_(2012)146-301]

Sergeants have never been an elite,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtassio,_Um_estudo_sociopolítico_sobre_a_formação_de_praças_(2012)70-302] but their training is a priority, albeit secondary to that of officers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCann,_The_Brazilian_Armed_Forces_(2017)77-303] A career sergeant is expected to have obedience to officers, critical thinking, greater culture and technical-professional preparation than his subordinates[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZimmermann,_Escolas_de_formação_e_aperfeiçoamento_dos_sargentos_(2022)436-437-304] and a robust professional identity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFerreira,_Uma_análise_dos_sistemas_simbólicos_da_Escola_de_Sargentos_das_Armas_(2022)371-300] The students at the Sergeants' Branches School are, for the most part, the children of civilians, but many have previous military experience, especially in mandatory military service. They are older than AMAN cadets (18–28 years old upon joining, most often in the 20–24 range) and from families with lower incomes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtassio,_Um_estudo_sociopolítico_sobre_a_formação_de_praças_(2012)94-121-305]

Officers and sergeants in a military organization come from all over the country and transfer throughout their career. Among sergeants, this is less frequent and over shorter distances.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCann,_The_Brazilian_Armed_Forces_(2017)77-303][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESilva,_O_exército_como_família_(2016)143-306] In his career, marked by training, specialization and improvement courses, the sergeant acquires professional stability after ten years of service. Having graduated as a third sergeant, he can rise to sub-lieutenant or captain of the Auxiliary Officer Cadre.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZimmermann,_Escolas_de_formação_e_aperfeiçoamento_dos_sargentos_(2022)449-451-307] There are also temporary sergeants who were privates that rose through military service[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarcos,_Capacitação_profissional_de_militares_temporários_(2001)49-308] and temporary technical sergeants, who are volunteers incorporated through an internship.[^cite_note-309]

### Corporals and privates

Main article: [Conscription in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscription_in_Brazil)



Young men presenting themselves for the selection phase of military service in 2014

Privates in combat units are incorporated through initial mandatory military service.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Razões_da_permanência_do_modelo_de_recrutamento_no_Brasil_(2001)72-310] Enlistment is mandatory for men the year they turn 18.[^cite_note-veja2-311] Of the three singular forces, the army is the one that most depends on compulsory military service. Conscripts are a considerable part of its force, unlike the navy and air force, in which they are a small minority.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Razões_da_permanência_do_modelo_de_recrutamento_no_Brasil_(2001)78-312][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHudson,_Brazil:_a_country_study_(1998)385-313] There are several alternatives to this type of military service,[^cite_note-314] including the *Tiros de Guerra*, dispersed in [municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Brazil) across the country, where reservist privates, known as *atiradores* (shooters), have part-time training.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCann,_The_Brazilian_Armed_Forces_(2017)75-76-315]

Military Service Boards, the lowest administrative level of conscription, are present throughout the entire country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)114-316] However, only in tributary municipalities, chosen for geographic and economic reasons, will enlisted men continue to the next stages of the process.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Razões_da_permanência_do_modelo_de_recrutamento_no_Brasil_(2001)82-83-317] Each military organization will receive recruits from the surrounding areas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCann,_The_Brazilian_Armed_Forces_(2017)74-318] By the end of the process, around 5% of those enlisted are sent into active service.[^cite_note-veja2-311][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Razões_da_permanência_do_modelo_de_recrutamento_no_Brasil_(2001)90-319] In 2012, 92% were volunteers, according to the Army.[^cite_note-320]

The official justifications for the system are the same as those made by its [defenders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sortition_Law#Support) (such as [Olavo Bilac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olavo_Bilac)) when compulsory military service was implemented at the beginning of the 20th century. According to [Enzo Martins Peri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enzo_Martins_Peri), the army commander in 2007–2015, the current model is "more democratic, universal and, socially, more fair and equitable" and complements the "formation of citizenship in the spiritual, moral, physical, intellectual, professional and civic aspects".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELima,_Serviço_militar_obrigatório_no_Brasil_(2010)59-60-321] The army seeks to mold recruits into an ideal of an orderly, productive and patriotic citizen and even undertakes professional qualification initiatives,[^cite_note-322] but this social function only applies to a small minority of enlisted personnel called up for service.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELima,_Serviço_militar_obrigatório_no_Brasil_(2010)72-74-323] In the case of the *Tiros de Guerra*, the low frequency of military exercises, leaving most of the time to cleaning, maintaining facilities and civic-social activities, can be a factor of frustration.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)217-324]



Recruit training at the 50th Jungle Infantry Battalion

The ideal of compulsory military service as a republican leveler, in which all social classes serve, does not correspond to reality.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELima,_Serviço_militar_obrigatório_no_Brasil_(2010)72-74-323] Young people are mostly recruited from low-income families,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESteffens,_Condições_de_vida_e_trabalho_do_recruta_(2016)11-325] for whom military service is an alternative to the lack of opportunities in the job market.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELima,_Serviço_militar_obrigatório_no_Brasil_(2010)116-326] A recruit's pay, however, is less than [minimum wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_wage).[^cite_note-327]

The recruits, or variable force privates (*Efetivo Variável*; EV), serve for one year,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESteffens,_Condições_de_vida_e_trabalho_do_recruta_(2016)23-328][^cite_note-329] during which they are meant to be transformed into a reservist fit for combat.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCann,_The_Brazilian_Armed_Forces_(2017)75-3] The recruit is only considered ready in the last part of the cycle, the training period, which lasts around four months. This creates an operational gap of two-thirds of the year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Razões_da_permanência_do_modelo_de_recrutamento_no_Brasil_(2001)92-330] One of the solutions is to vary the schedules so that, throughout the year, some units are operational and others are not. It is not clear in which part of the year the entire army would be trained and ready.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCann,_The_Brazilian_Armed_Forces_(2017)75-3]

Privates are temporary personnel and do not pursue a career.[^cite_note-331] Volunteers interested in serving for more than twelve months can re-engage, that is, extend their time of service, depending on vacancies and the army's judgment. The reengaged private moves from the EV to the Base Core (*Núcleo Base*; NB), which also includes career military personnel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarcos,_Capacitação_profissional_de_militares_temporários_(2001)34-332] NB privates undertake professional training and advancement courses, and may be promoted to corporal or temporary sergeant.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDenardin_et_al.,_Sentido_do_trabalho_e_a_escolha_pela_vida_na_caserna_(2022)85-333] Re-engagements can be requested annually up to a maximum of 96 months of service.[^cite_note-dsm2-204][^cite_note-334] The goal set in 1998 was to have a workforce of 75% NB and 25% EV.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Razões_da_permanência_do_modelo_de_recrutamento_no_Brasil_(2001)91-335] Some brigades have more professional personnel;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCann,_The_Brazilian_Armed_Forces_(2017)75-3][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Estrutura_militar_e_ordenamento_político_(2007)162-336] the Parachute Infantry Brigade is made up entirely of volunteers,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Estrutura_militar_e_ordenamento_político_(2007)1-337] and the 26th Parachute Infantry Battalion is made up exclusively of NB personnel.[^cite_note-338]

### Women



Female army soldier

Women are exempt from mandatory military service in Brazil, but may volunteer as career or temporary military personnel.[^cite_note-339] The Brazilian Army was the first in South America to allow women as career soldiers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHudson,_Brazil:_a_country_study_(1998)398-340]

The first formal participation of women in the Brazilian Army was in 1943, with FEB nurses. Female participation returned in 1992, following changes in society and the opening of the navy and air force to women. Initially occupying administrative and technical positions in QCO, throughout the decade women were also admitted to the Health Staff, the Military Engineers Staff and the temporary officers and sergeants staff.[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchwether,_Processo_de_incorporação_das_mulheres_nas_Forças_Armadas_de_Brasil_e_Argentina_(2016)97-103-342]

EsPCEx welcomed its first female students in 2017, opening the military line to women, but they can only choose the Quartermaster and Ordnance branches. The army plans to open the communications branch to women in 2024. Women made up about 6% of active personnel in 2023.[^cite_note-galdeano-343]

### Reserve



Delivery of army armored vehicles to the [Military Police of Rio de Janeiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Police_of_Rio_de_Janeiro_State), one of its auxiliary and reserve forces

At the end of active service, soldiers join the [reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_reserve_force), from where they can still be called up in a war or emergency.[^cite_note-344] Officers and NCOs with at least 30 years of service, known as R1 military personnel, join the paid reserve.[^cite_note-345] Only upon reaching an age limit or physical incapacity is a paid reserve officer or NCO released from mobilization and, instead of a reservist, becomes a *reformado*.[^cite_note-346] Ostracism and difficulty adjusting to civilian life are among the army's reasons for financing its pensioners and reservists.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos_&_Raposo,_A_elite_do_Exército_(2019)63-347]

Temporary military personnel are part of the unpaid reserve. For five years after the end of service, the "availability" period, they are subject to the Reserve Presentation Exercise (*Exercício de Apresentação da Reserva*; EXAR).[^cite_note-348] Every year, a certain number of reservists are called up to renew their skills and participate in an operation. According to historian [Frank McCann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_McCann), the reserve is immense, but there has never been a full call-up, and therefore, it is not known whether it would be successful. There is no organized reserve like in the United States, with reserve units responsible for preserving the skills of these soldiers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCann,_The_Brazilian_Armed_Forces_(2017)75-3]

In principle, the 1988 Brazilian constitution designates the [Military Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Police_\(Brazil\)) (*Polícias Militares*; PMs) and Military Fire Brigades (*Corpos de Bombeiros Militares*; CBMs) as auxiliary and reserve forces of the army, although in practice they remain separate entities, at state rather than federal scope. The military police, unlike the Armed Forces, deal with the Brazilian population and not with an external enemy.[^cite_note-349] The army controls these institutions through the General Inspectorate of Military Police (*Inspetoria Geral das Polícias Militares*; IGPM). The army's interference in the PM's internal affairs has reduced since the end of the military dictatorship, but it still has veto powers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMedeiros,_Aspectos_institucionais_da_unificação_das_polícias_no_Brasil_(2004)271-350] The IISS includes the military police in the category of "gendarmerie & paramilitary", estimating 395 thousand men.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIISS,_The_Military_Balance_(2023)386-351]

## Traditions and culture



Army medals

The army clings to its values and identity even as it modernizes; in this sense, it is a conservative institution. Incorporating individuals from society transforms them more than they transform it.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)192-193-352] Lieutenant colonel and sociologist Everton Araújo dos Santos defined it: "conservative, traditional, hermetic, austere, reserved and averse to investigations", having "very well delineated contours of the social roles to be played by each of the individuals who compose it".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos_&_Raposo,_A_elite_do_Exército_(2019)60-353] The army can also be conservative externally, in relation to civil society and the State.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)228-229-354]

Military values are based on a distinction between the "inside" and "outside" worlds; the military world is considered more organized, honest, and patriotic than the civilian one.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECastro,_Antropologia_dos_militares_no_Brasil_(2015)3-4-355] According to the Military Statute, the "essential manifestations of military value" are patriotism, "civic virtue and the cult of historical traditions", "faith in the elevated mission of the Armed Forces", *esprit de corps*, love for the profession and technical-professional improvement.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEViana,_História_institucional_e_cultura_organizacional_da_tropa_aeroterrestre_(2020)319-356]

To protect itself from external influences, the army offers services so that "upon entering it, its member \[can], within and throughout his life, in isolation and protected in a familiar and sufficient world, see a good part of his needs satisfied, and those of his dependents". The army's own healthcare system, FUSEx, covers all healthcare needs and is mandatory for military personnel and pensioners to participate in. When traveling, officers, sergeants, and dependents can be accommodated in army transit hotels. Catholic priests and evangelical pastors are assimilated as officers to cover religious service. There are leisure areas exclusive to the "internal public", such as the Imbuí beach, in [Niterói](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niter%C3%B3i). It is possible to reach generalship by attending, from childhood, only military institutions,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_das_relações_pessoais_dos_militares_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2012)240-244-357] and the highest priority of military education is moral training, thus serving as yet another protection mechanism.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)263,_275-358]

To reinforce established practices and behaviors, giving them the respectability of the past, and to assign an identity to the army and specific units, the institution upholds traditions, usually symbols, rites, and ceremonies. These include the cult of "patrons", the order of precedence between branches, cadres, and services, the noble or honor halls, the galleries of former commanders, the "historical denominations" of military organizations, the army's motto[^cite_note-359] and the designation of the First Battle of Guararapes as the landmark of the force's founding.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Tradições_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2022)722-723-360]

### Patrons and dates



Bust and sword of Caxias on Soldier's Day

[Luís Alves de Lima e Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lu%C3%ADs_Alves_de_Lima_e_Silva,_Duke_of_Caxias), the Duke of Caxias, is the "maximum representation of authority, order and tradition for the Brazilian Army".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)257-361] On the centennial of his death, in 1980, Army Minister Walter Pires called him the "personification of the army itself" and a "perfect model of a soldier". Caxias is designated "patron of the army," and his birthday on 25 August is celebrated as "Soldier's Day." The "cult" of Caxias was made official in 1923 and promoted by military authorities as a symbol of a disciplined and loyal soldier. Until then, the main celebration was 24 May, the anniversary of the [Battle of Tuyutí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Tuyut%C3%AD), and Caxias was less celebrated than [Manuel Luís Osório](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Lu%C3%ADs_Os%C3%B3rio,_Marquis_of_Erval).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECastro,_Entre_Caxias_e_Osório_(2000)-362]

At a level below Caxias, the branches, cadres and services have their own patrons, such as [Antônio de Sampaio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ant%C3%B4nio_de_Sampaio), patron of the infantry, [Villagran Cabrita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_Carlos_de_Villagran_Cabrita), patron of engineering, and [Emílio Luís Mallet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89mile_Mallet,_Baron_of_Itapevi), patron of artillery.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECastro,_Patronos_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2022)-363] Sampaio's birthday, for example, is "Infantry Day".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECastro,_Entre_Caxias_e_Osório_(2000)-362] Some specialties and social circles, such as special forces, aviation, and the military family, also have designated patrons. All are historical figures used as examples of attitudes and virtues necessary for military activity. The cult of these figures was consolidated in the 1960s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECastro,_Patronos_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2022)-363]

The only date with equivalent importance to Soldier's Day is the Army's Day on 19 April, the anniversary of the First Battle of Guararapes. This celebration evokes the belief that the army was born with the Brazilian nation, comprises the "three races that form the essence of the Brazilian people," and defeated a more powerful foreign invader through guerrilla warfare and ambushes. The commemoration of Guararapes can also be associated with the defense of the Amazon and the "resistance strategy".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECastro,_O_espírito_de_Guararapes_(2002)-20]

### Symbology



3rd Guard Cavalry Regiment flying the banners of military commands

The Brazilian Army has visual symbols — coats of arms, flags, banners, and pennants — and musical ones — drumbeats, cornets and bugles, songs, shouts, and war choruses.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Símbolos_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2022)674-364] In parades, its dominant musical genre is the *dobrado*, played by [military bands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_band) and [fanfare bands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fanfare_band).[^cite_note-365] There are hymns and songs of the army, branches, cadres and services, military organizations, and traditional songs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Hinos_e_canções_militares_(2022)-366] Likewise, specific troops have their own battlecries. The most traditional are "*Selva*", from the jungle warriors, and "*Brasil acima de tudo*" ("Brazil above everything"), which originated among paratroopers and later disseminated.[^cite_note-367] The army's visual identity includes a coat of arms, a banner with the coat of arms and a symbol, the "elliptical cockade" with concentric national colors. It first appeared in uniform regulations in 1951 and has been applied to vehicles since the 1990s, replacing the [Southern Cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Cross) inscribed in a circle that had been used since World War II.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Símbolos_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2022)675-676-368]

Some military organizations carry banners, which are their "war flags", and all have badges. Commanders, chiefs, and directors are identified by insignia flags. Branches, cadres, and services have their own heraldic symbols and colors.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Símbolos_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2022)676-678-369] Organizations with banners have "historical names" referring to dates, places or characters related to the history of the organization or its predecessors, such as the "5th Light Infantry Battalion – [Itororó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Ytoror%C3%B3) Regiment".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Tradições_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2022)724-370]

## Organization



The Brazilian Army Headquarters in Brasília

As part of the Brazilian Armed Forces, the Brazilian Army has its superior direction exercised by the Ministry of Defense and its [Supreme Commander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief) is the [President of Brazil](273638.md), who appoints an army commander suggested by the Minister of Defense. The army's structure, complex and well-demarcated territorially, is centered on the Army Command, which has functional autonomy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)62-371] The Army Headquarters is located in the Urban Military Sector, in [Brasília](4752.md), the capital of Brazil.[^cite_note-372]

The Army Command has a General Management Body, the Army General Staff (*Estado-Maior do Exército*; EME), four Superior Advisory Bodies,[^cite_note-373] six Direct and Immediate Assistance Bodies,[^cite_note-374] an Operational Management Body, the Land Operations Command (*Comando de Operações Terrestres*; COTER), and six Sector Management Bodies (*Órgãos de Direção Setorial*; ODS).[^cite_note-375]

The operational arm of the Army is the Land Force.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELivro_Branco_de_Defesa_Nacional_(2020)75-376] COTER, created to relieve the EME of the executive aspects of operations,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENunes,_Debate_teórico_e_inferências_a_partir_do_caso_da_Doutrina_Delta_(2023)114-377] evaluates the operational capacity and instruction of the Land Force. The EME formulates military doctrine,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Estrutura_militar_e_ordenamento_político_(2007)113-378] strategic planning and general action guidelines.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)71-72-379] The [Army High Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_High_Command) (*Alto Comando do Exército*; ACE), made up of army generals,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESantos,_Um_estudo_da_família_militar_(2018)278-291] is only a superior advisory body, but it was a general management body, coexisting with the EME, until 2006.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Estrutura_militar_e_ordenamento_político_(2007)112-380] The [Brazilian War Materiel Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMBEL) (*Indústria de Material Bélico do Brasil*; Imbel) is linked to the Army Command.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERelatório_de_Gestão_do_Comando_do_Exército_(2023)21-381]

Several ODS have technical links with Land Force units.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)70-382] The Department of Science and Technology (*Departamento de Ciência e Tecnologia*; DCT) is also linked to Imbel and has the Army Technological Center (*Centro Tecnológico do Exército*; CTEx) in its structure,[^cite_note-383] a research body responsible for projects such as the [Saber Radar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saber_Radar), ALAC and [MSS-1.2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MSS-1.2).[^cite_note-384]



Brazilian Army organization in 2026 (click to enlarge)

### Commands, divisions and regions

The Land Force is made up of eight Military Area Commands, each responsible for a portion of Brazil's territory.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELivro_Branco_de_Defesa_Nacional_(2020)77-385] When created, the territorial subdivision had a certain similarity to [IBGE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Institute_of_Geography_and_Statistics)'s 1969 [regionalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regions_of_Brazil).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)63-386] Military Area Commands evolved from the four numbered [armies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_army) and two area commands that existed until 1985.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHudson,_Brazil:_a_country_study_(1998)379-387] They encompass large operational commands, the [Army Divisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Division_\(military\)) (*Divisões de Exército*; DEs), and large administrative, logistical and territorial commands, the [Military Regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_district) (*Regiões Militares*; RMs).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELivro_Branco_de_Defesa_Nacional_(2020)77-385] Some RMs command combatant military organizations, but this is not the norm.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)66-388] DEs are brigade coordination commands and do not have a fixed constitution.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Modernização_e_reestruturação_do_Exército_(2018)170-389]

### Brigades and units







From top to bottom, the 16th Motorized Infantry Battalion, 13th Mechanized Cavalry Regiment and 7th Armored Infantry Battalion

According to the Brazilian Army's doctrine, the [brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigade) is the "basic large unit (*Grande Unidade*; GU) of [combined arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combined_arms)", "with the capacity to act independently and last in action". Its main components are infantry battalions and cavalry regiments,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPestana,_Pelotão_de_reconhecimento_de_montanha_(2019)12-395] which are supported by [command](7092305.md), field artillery, logistics, [reconnaissance](146911.md) and security, [anti-aircraft artillery](146640.md), combat engineering, communications, and [Army Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_Police_\(Brazil\)) elements.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Modernização_e_reestruturação_do_Exército_(2018)172-176-396] Each type of brigade has a fixed formation,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Modernização_e_reestruturação_do_Exército_(2018)170-389] but the actual formation may differ from the doctrinal one, and several brigades are incomplete.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Organização_das_Forças_do_Exército_Brasileiro_na_República_(2022)391-392-397] A brigade's strength can be less than 2,000 soldiers or more than 5,000.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERelatório_de_Gestão_do_Comando_do_Exército_(2021)13-398]

The Armored Cavalry and Armored Infantry brigades are heavy brigades.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELivro_Branco_de_Defesa_Nacional_(2020)79-399] Their composition is identical, with two tank regiments and two [armored infantry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mechanized_infantry) battalions as its main components.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouza,_As_forças_blindadas_do_Exército_Brasileiro_(2010)57-400] The Mechanized Cavalry and Mechanized Infantry brigades are medium brigades;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELivro_Branco_de_Defesa_Nacional_(2020)79-399] a Mechanized Cavalry Brigade has two regiments of mechanized cavalry and one of [armored cavalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armoured_cavalry),[^cite_note-401] while the Mechanized Infantry Brigade, with three battalions of mechanized infantry, can be compared to the United States Army's [Stryker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stryker) Brigade Combat Team.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENakashima,_A_Infantaria_Mecanizada_brasileira_e_a_norte-americana_(2021)-402] The others (Motorized Infantry, Light Infantry, Parachute Infantry, Jungle Infantry and Pantanal Infantry) are light brigades,[^cite_note-403] consisting of three infantry battalions of various types.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Modernização_e_reestruturação_do_Exército_(2018)215-161]

Brigades are subordinate to an Army Division or a Military Area Command. In an equivalent situation, there are Divisional Artillery, Engineering Groups and commands for specific use: [Aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Army_Aviation), [Special Operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Operations_Command_\(Brazil\)), Anti-Air Defense, Artillery, Communications and Electronic Warfare and Cyber Defense.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELivro_Branco_de_Defesa_Nacional_(2020)79-399]

[Battalions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battalion) (in infantry, engineering and communications), [regiments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regiment) (in cavalry), [groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_\(military_unit\)) (in artillery), parks (in Ordnance) and depots (in the Quartermaster Service) are called "units", consisting of a single branch and commanded by a colonel or lieutenant colonel. In combat branches, units have 500 to 900 soldiers. These include "subunits": [companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Company_\(military_unit\)) (in infantry, engineering or communications), [squadrons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Squadron_\(army\)) (in cavalry), [batteries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artillery_battery) (in artillery) and [flights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flight_\(military_unit\)) (in aviation). Some subunits are independent, with their own administration.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedrosa,_Modernização_e_reestruturação_do_Exército_(2018)21-404][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEViana,_História_institucional_e_cultura_organizacional_da_tropa_aeroterrestre_(2020)298-405]

### Geographic distribution



Location of brigades in 2024 and their respective employment groups

The distribution of troops across Brazil's territory follows strategy and keeps remnants of past guidelines.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)95-406] The country is widely dotted with barracks,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)76-407] even on the most remote frontiers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERodrigues_&_Medeiros,_Exército_Brasileiro_(2022)497-408] Construction engineering battalions work on various road and rail routes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlores,_O_desdobramento_das_Organizações_Militares_de_Engenharia_de_Construção_(2017)27-409] Throughout history, each reorganization and increase in personnel filled more spaces, as recommended by the "Presence Strategy". This differs, for example, from the [Chilean Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chilean_Army), which has become compact and mobile since the 1990s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERodrigues,_A_Estratégia_da_Presença_(2020)66-410] Still, the Brazilian Army has strategic reaction forces in the "central core" of the country, between [São Paulo](390875.md), [Rio de Janeiro](25936.md) and [Brasília](4752.md), from where they can access any other region.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Estrutura_militar_e_ordenamento_político_(2007)129-411]

The greatest concentration of troops is in the [Center-South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centro-Sul), despite the priority given to the Amazon's defense. The centers of political ([Federal District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_District_\(Brazil\))) and economic power (São Paulo and Rio de Janeiro) and some border states ([Rio Grande do Sul](256268.md), [Paraná](229411.md), [Mato Grosso do Sul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mato_Grosso_do_Sul) and [Amazonas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazonas_\(Brazilian_state\))) have greater numbers of combat, logistics and administrative support units.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGonzales,_Os_Tiros_de_Guerra_e_a_estratégia_de_presença_(2008)76-79-412] Historically, the most politically important garrison was that of Rio de Janeiro, as the country's capital was there until 1960; furthermore, the main military schools are in that state. The other historical area of troop concentration is in the [South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Region,_Brazil), targeting the [border with Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentina%E2%80%93Brazil_border), Brazil's traditional rival on the continent.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHudson,_Brazil:_a_country_study_(1998)379-380-413] Rio de Janeiro lost several brigades from the 1990s onwards,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Estrutura_militar_e_ordenamento_político_(2007)123-414] while the [Southern Military Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Military_Command_\(Brazil\)) maintained its primacy. In 2011, it commanded 90% of the army's armor, 100% of its [self-propelled artillery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-propelled_artillery), 75% of its engineering units and 75% of its mechanized cavalry.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETeixeira,_A_cooperação_em_defesa_na_América_do_Sul_(2013)160-415]

### Employment groups and Readiness Forces



Specialized strategic employment forces (Aviation Command and Special Operations Command)



General employment force (6th Armored Infantry Brigade)

The Land Force is divided into three "employment groups": the General Employment Forces, which reinforce the others, the Immediate Employment Forces, which operate along the border, and the Strategic Employment Forces, which can move anywhere in the country and are "forces with combat power that enable, in situations of crisis/armed conflict, strategic imbalance, through deterrent and offensive strategies". There are also support modules and Specialized Strategic Employment Forces.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEConcepção_Estratégica_do_Exército_(2023)2-3_a_2-4-416] In the distribution of materiel and personnel, priority belongs to the Strategic Deployment Forces and some General Deployment ones.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEConcepção_Estratégica_do_Exército_(2023)2-7-417]

The employment groups were defined in 2017[^cite_note-418] and replace older categorizations, such as the "Strategic Action Forces" and, before them, the "Strategic Rapid Action Forces" (*Forças de Ação Rápida Estratégica*; FAR Estrt),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESoares,_Estudo_comparativo_de_modelos_de_Forças_de_Prontidão_(2021)59-419] understood as the strategic reserve of the army, highly mobile and fully trained. A number of companies and battalions were designated as regional FAR.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Estrutura_militar_e_ordenamento_político_(2007)128-130-420] The other forces, known as "Operational Military Organizations", would have greater shortages of personnel and materiel, and would be mainly responsible for forming the reserve.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPiletti,_O_exército_brasileiro_e_as_ameaças_não-tradicionais_(2008)80-421] Until the 1990s, the Parachute Infantry Brigade was the only strategic ready-deployment force.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Estrutura_militar_e_ordenamento_político_(2007)117-422]

The Strategic Employment Forces, Specialized Strategic Employment Forces, priority General Deployment Forces and support modules make up the Readiness Forces (*Forças de Prontidão*; FORPRON),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEConcepção_Estratégica_do_Exército_(2023)2-6-423] which maintain a rotation of professional personnel.[^cite_note-424] FORPRON are made up of unit-sized forces[^cite_note-425] provided by each of the brigades, with a total strength of 9,150 soldiers in 2023.[^cite_note-426] The Brazilian Army plans to have, in total, a division made up of two brigades from the FORPRON.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESoares,_Estudo_comparativo_de_modelos_de_Forças_de_Prontidão_(2021)69-427] From the FORPRON, the army can select an expeditionary force or peacekeeping force to send abroad.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVerde-Oliva,_FORPRON_(2022)12-428]

### Specialized components

#### Special Operations

Main article: [Special Operations Command (Brazil)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Operations_Command_\(Brazil\))



Special Operations soldiers

The army's special operations involve "highly specialized training of the combatant, the use of unconventional weapons and equipment, missions with high political sensitivity, the heterodox application of the principles of war, and the exploitation of limited opportunities", in [irregular warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irregular_warfare) missions, direct action, strategic reconnaissance, [counterinsurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterinsurgency) warfare, [counterterrorism](83392.md), [psychological operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychological_warfare), [search and rescue](172599.md), and [humanitarian assistance](434679.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPiletti,_O_exército_brasileiro_e_as_ameaças_não-tradicionais_(2008)82-83-429] Forces with these missions are part of the Special Operations Command (*Comando de Operações Especiais*; C Op Esp), based in [Goiânia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goi%C3%A2nia) and subordinate to the [Planalto Military Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planalto_Military_Command), with preparation and employment links to COTER. The exception is the 3rd Special Forces Company (Force 3), in Manaus, which is subordinate to the [Amazon Military Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_Military_Command), but linked to C Op Esp.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOliveira,_O_Comando_de_Operações_Especiais_(2020)34-430][^cite_note-431]

Its main units are the 1st Commando Actions Battalion (*1.º Batalhão de Ações de Comandos*; BAC) and the [1st Special Forces Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1%C2%BA_Batalh%C3%A3o_de_For%C3%A7as_Especiais) (*1.º Batalhão de Forças Especiais*; 1st BFEsp);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOliveira,_O_Comando_de_Operações_Especiais_(2020)32-432] both are elite units and difficult to join.[^cite_note-433] The training of 1st BFEsp personnel, also called "FEs" or "ghosts", is comparable to the American [Special Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Army_Special_Forces) and includes long patrols in the [Amazon jungle](48139.md) and urban crime fighting strategies.[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERyan_et_al.,_The_encyclopedia_of_the_world's_special_forces_(2014)-435]

#### By terrain







From top to bottom: the jungle infantry, 17th Border Battalion and 72nd Caatinga Infantry Battalion

Present in all [biomes of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biomes_in_Brazil), the army has specialized troops for mountain, [Pantanal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pantanal), [caatinga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caatinga), jungle[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESales,_A_gestão_ambiental_no_Exército_Brasileiro_(2020)23-436] (which, in the army's context refers to the [Amazon](48139.md)),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPiletti,_O_exército_brasileiro_e_as_ameaças_não-tradicionais_(2008)78-437] and urban environments.[^cite_note-campineiro-438] Specialization courses and internships in these environments are carried out in some battalions or in instruction centers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Estrutura_militar_e_ordenamento_político_(2007)133-136-439]

Jungle training largely occurs in military organizations in the Amazon, but there is a specialized training center, the Jungle Warfare Instruction Center (*Centro de Instrução de Guerra na Selva*; CIGS), in Manaus. With extreme physical and mental demands, it is highly competitive[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPiletti,_O_exército_brasileiro_e_as_ameaças_não-tradicionais_(2008)78-437] and internationally renowned.[^cite_note-440][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCann,_The_Brazilian_Armed_Forces_(2017)65-441] The Brazilian jungle infantry is characterized by survival in a hostile environment, decentralized fractions, river and air movements and flanking maneuvers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPiletti,_O_exército_brasileiro_e_as_ameaças_não-tradicionais_(2008)75-442][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGomes_&_Senhoras,_Estudo_de_caso_da_1ª_Brigada_de_Infantaria_de_Selva_(2021)24-443] Brazilian army theorists recognize that the country would not resist an invasion of the Amazon by a superior military force through conventional means. Inspired by the [Vietnam War](32611.md) and their experience in Araguaia, they propose guerrilla warfare to wear down the invader's will,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCann,_The_Brazilian_Armed_Forces_(2017)71-444] in the so-called "Resistance Strategy".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPiletti,_O_exército_brasileiro_e_as_ameaças_não-tradicionais_(2008)64-445]

These specialized forces comprise the six Jungle Infantry Brigades deployed in the Amazon: the 1st in [Boa Vista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boa_Vista,_Roraima), 2nd in [São Gabriel da Cachoeira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Gabriel_da_Cachoeira), 16th in [Tefé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tef%C3%A9), 17th in [Porto Velho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porto_Velho), 22nd in [Macapá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macap%C3%A1) and 23rd in [Marabá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marab%C3%A1,_Par%C3%A1).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGomes_&_Senhoras,_Estudo_de_caso_da_1ª_Brigada_de_Infantaria_de_Selva_(2021)30-32-446] Some of its Jungle Infantry Battalions (*Batalhões de Infantaria de Selva*; BIS) are designated "Frontier Commands", encompassing Special Border Companies and Platoons (*Companhias Especiais de Fronteira*; CEF, and *Pelotões Especiais de Fronteira*; PEF); although essential in surveillance, these platoons have very limited military power.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPiletti,_O_exército_brasileiro_e_as_ameaças_não-tradicionais_(2008)74-75,_78-447] Recruits at border posts are largely local indigenous people, but there are tensions with the local population.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCann,_The_Brazilian_Armed_Forces_(2017)69-448]

Military mountaineering instruction takes place at the 4th Light Mountain Infantry Brigade, in [Juiz de Fora](882285.md),[^cite_note-meireles2-449] and does not include snow operations, as in mountain environments in other countries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuhlmann,_Estrutura_militar_e_ordenamento_político_(2007)135-450] Internships in Pantanal operations take place at the 17th Border Battalion, in [Corumbá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corumb%C3%A1).[^cite_note-451] Employing waterway and air transport, it continuously patrols the border and is subordinate to the 18th Pantanal Infantry Brigade.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERodrigues,_As_ameaças_no_Arco_Central_da_Faixa_de_Fronteira_(2023)37-452]

The Caatinga Operations Instruction Center (*Centro de Instrução de Operações na Caatinga*; CIOpC) is part of the 72nd Caatinga Infantry Battalion,[^cite_note-453] in [Petrolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrolina), [Pernambuco](229525.md). Caatinga fighters are distinguished by their reinforced leather uniforms, to protect themselves from thorny vegetation.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455] The Urban Operations Instruction Center is part of the 28th Mechanized Infantry Battalion, in [Campinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campinas).[^cite_note-456][^cite_note-campineiro-438]

#### By air transport



Parachute jump



Airmobile infantry with Army Aviation

The [Parachute Infantry Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paratroopers_Brigade_\(Brazil\)) and the [12th Light Infantry Brigade (Airmobile)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/12th_Light_Infantry_Brigade_\(Airmobile\)) are the two brigades listed by the IISS in the "aerial maneuver" category, that is, trained and equipped to reach the battlefield by transport aircraft and/or helicopters.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIISS,_The_Military_Balance_(2023)383,_494-457] Outside the brigades there is another airmobile unit, the 1st Jungle Infantry Battalion, in Manaus.[^cite_note-458] Mountain infantry, as it is also light infantry, may be airlifted.[^cite_note-meireles2-449]

The airmobile brigade specializes in helitransport operations (along with Army Aviation), but as it is light in equipment and weapons, it can also be transported in air force planes or civilian vehicles such as vans, buses, trucks, trains and ships. Its operational concept is focused on [infiltration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infiltration_tactics), [extraction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extraction_\(military\)) and airmobile assault behind enemy lines, employing Army Aviation helicopters.[^cite_note-459] Subordinated to the 2nd Army Division, the brigade is headquartered in [Caçapava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ca%C3%A7apava), São Paulo,[^cite_note-cmse-393] in the [Rio-São Paulo axis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_de_Janeiro%E2%80%93S%C3%A3o_Paulo_megalopolis) region, where it is close to the Army Aviation Command.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFalconi,_Aviação_naval_brasileira_(2009)197-460] Army Aviation has two battalions in [Taubaté](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taubat%C3%A9), one in Campo Grande, one in Manaus and a detachment in Belém.[^cite_note-461]

The parachute brigade, in turn, is focused on airborne assault, although it has never carried out a mass parachute jump outside of exercises and maneuvers. Its great usefulness lies in the immediate transport of its troops, and its recent employment situations tend to be in an urban environment.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEViana,_História_institucional_e_cultura_organizacional_da_tropa_aeroterrestre_(2020)348-462] Its official mission is to "move quickly \[...] by airlift or airdrop", arriving within 48 hours at any point in Brazil. Paratroopers are considered an elite troop within the army[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBruno,_Narrativa_e_construção_de_identidades:_o_combatente_pára-quedista_do_Exército_(2010)52-463] and a "showcase" of the institution.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEViana,_História_institucional_e_cultura_organizacional_da_tropa_aeroterrestre_(2020)348-462]

The brigade receives many more volunteers than it has vacancies,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEViana,_História_institucional_e_cultura_organizacional_da_tropa_aeroterrestre_(2020)22-464] it is more rigorous in selection[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEViana,_História_institucional_e_cultura_organizacional_da_tropa_aeroterrestre_(2020)318-465] and cultivates a "paratrooper mystique" and its own identity and values.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBruno,_Narrativa_e_construção_de_identidades:_o_combatente_pára-quedista_do_Exército_(2010)179-180-466] Some traditions visually distinguish paratroopers, such as brown combat boots, red berets and silver wings chevrons. Headquartered in Rio de Janeiro, the brigade is subordinate to the Land Operations Command and the Eastern Military Command.[^cite_note-467]

#### Chemical, Biological, Radiological and Nuclear Defense



DQBRN soldiers in counterterrorism training

[Chemical, Biological, Radiological and Nuclear defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBRN_defense) (*Defesa Química, Biológica, Radiológica e Nuclear*; DQBRN) is carried out by the 1st DBQRN Battalion (subordinated to the Eastern Military Command), the DQBRN Company (subordinated to the Special Operations Command), the School of Specialized Instruction and the DQBRN Institute of the Army's Technological Center, in coordination with its counterparts in the Armed Forces, the [National Nuclear Energy Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Nuclear_Energy_Commission) and international organizations such as the [International Atomic Energy Agency](14984.md) and the [Organization for the Prohibition of Chemical Weapons](36832.md). They can be used in response to accidents (their first real use was in the [Goiânia accident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goi%C3%A2nia_accident) in 1987), terrorist attacks[^cite_note-cia_dqbrn-468][^cite_note-469] and in a war environment, to recover forces attacked by such weapons of mass destruction. To this end, they perform the recognition, identification and decontamination of QBRN threats.[^cite_note-470][^cite_note-471]

#### Army Police

Main article: [Army Police (Brazil)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_Police_\(Brazil\))



Army police parade

The Army Police (*Polícia do Exército*; PE) is, in the generic sense, the [military police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_police) of the Brazilian Army, and should not be confused with the state police forces (*Polícias Militares*).[^cite_note-472] Its soldiers are identified by an armband with the letters "PE". It is a specialized infantry troop available at each level of command: Military Area Commands have PE battalions, divisions have companies and brigades have platoons. The PE monitors troop discipline, prevents and investigates crime, provides security for authorities, guards prisoners and patrols facilities, among other duties. It is used both in conventional military operations and in combating crime.[^cite_note-473] Current PE doctrine recognizes broad and hybrid action in "humanized terrains (urban or rural)", in which there are "actors acting in spaces that go beyond the battlefield" and multiple dimensions — physical, human and informational — of the operational environment.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlves,_Limites_espaciais_para_a_atuação_do_Exército_Brasileiro_no_policiamento_urbano_(2021)22-25-474]

#### Guard infantry and cavalry



BGP and Independence Dragoons at the Palácio do Planalto

"Military guard organizations" belong to the infantry and cavalry and are dedicated to security and military ceremonies. In law and order operations, they can carry out search and arrest, overt patrolling and riot control, among other activities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEManual_de_Campanha,_Organizações_Militares_de_Guardas_(2021)p._1-1,_3-3_a_3-6-475] The three guard cavalry regiments are the last ones still mounted on horseback in Brazil.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESavian,_Resistências_à_mecanização_(2013)15-476]

Two guard units alternate security at the ramp at [Palácio do Planalto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pal%C3%A1cio_do_Planalto), Brazil's presidential workplace: the [Presidential Guard Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Guard_Battalion_\(Brazil\)) (*Batalhão da Guarda Presidencial*; BGP) and the [1st Guard Cavalry Regiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Dragoons), known as the "Independence Dragoons". The BGP has guard and ceremonial duties for the president, foreign heads of state and the diplomatic corps.[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478] During demonstrations, it acts as [riot police](1148542.md), armed with shields, batons, pepper spray and rubber bullets.[^cite_note-479][^cite_note-480]

## Equipment

Main article: [List of modern equipment of the Brazilian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_modern_equipment_of_the_Brazilian_Army)

### Armored vehicles



From left to right: ASTROS II, Gepard, M109, Leopard 1, VBTP-MR Guarani and M113

In the Brazilian Army, forces centered on [armored combat vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armoured_fighting_vehicle) are classified as "armored" if their vehicles move on tracks and "mechanized" if they use wheels.[^cite_note-481] The "backbone" of armored forces and cavalry is the [Leopard 1A5 BR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopard_1#Leopard_1A5) tank.[^cite_note-leopard_frota-482][^cite_note-leopard_cisne2-483] In 2023, the army operated 220 units of this model, 41 of the Leopard 1A1 BE and 35 of the [M60 A3 TTS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M60_tank), for a total of 296 main battle tanks (MBTs). They are complemented by 660 tracked [armored personnel carriers](19278686.md) (APCs) of various [M-113](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M113_armored_personnel_carrier) and [M577A2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M577A2) models, at least seven [armored engineering vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armored_engineering_vehicle), thirteen [recovery vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armoured_recovery_vehicle), and five [bridge launchers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armoured_vehicle-launched_bridge).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIISS,_The_Military_Balance_(2023)384-484] Armored brigades have no [infantry fighting vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infantry_fighting_vehicle) (IFVs).[^cite_note-485] Mechanized forces have around 575 [Guarani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VBTP-MR_Guarani) and 46 [EE-11 Urutu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EE-11_Urutu) APCs, 408 [EE-9 Cascavel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EE-9_Cascavel) [armored reconnaissance vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armored_reconnaissance_vehicle) and 32 [Guaicurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iveco_LMV) light mobility vehicles. The IISS includes 13 Guaranis armed with 30 mm guns as the Brazilian Army's only IFVs.[^cite_note-486][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIISS,_The_Military_Balance_(2024)417-487]

Quantitatively, the Brazilian Army has the largest fleet of MBTs in South America. However, only the Chilean Army has modern MBTs, according to the IISS classification.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIISS,_The_Military_Balance_(2023)365-488] This condition of numerical advantage, in tanks and in armored vehicles in general, is the same as that observed in numbers from the 2000s and early 2010s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIISS,_The_Military_Balance_(2024)397-489][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERezende,_Brasil:_ator_unipolar_na_América_do_Sul?_(2016)286-287-490][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDullius,_Gastos_militares_na_América_do_Sul_(2008)12-14-491] Qualitatively, the region's armies, with the exception of Chile's, operated armored vehicles designed in the 1960s and 1970s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDullius,_Gastos_militares_na_América_do_Sul_(2008)13-492] The Leopard 1A5, for example, has technology from the 1980s, but was purchased by Brazil in the 2000s.[^cite_note-leopard_cisne2-483] The spread of anti-tank missiles in the armies of neighboring countries is already a threat to Brazilian armored forces.[^cite_note-493] The Armored Forces Subprogram Implementation Guideline, in 2020, recognized that the majority of the armored vehicle fleet was already close to the end of its life cycle.[^cite_note-494]

### Artillery



Towed M114 howitzers



M109 self-propelled howitzer



Gepard anti-aircraft system

The artillery branch is subdivided into groups and batteries of [field artillery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_artillery), missile and [rocket artillery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocket_artillery) and [anti-aircraft artillery](146640.md).[^cite_note-artilharia3-495][^cite_note-496] The IISS estimated a total of 1,881 pieces of field and missile and rocket artillery in 2023: 1,245 mortars (of which 1,168 are 81 mm caliber and the rest 120 mm), 431 towed artillery pieces (336 105 mm and 95 155 mm), 169 pieces of 155 mm [self-propelled artillery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-propelled_artillery) and 36 missile and rocket launchers. However, there are smaller numbers of several models.[^cite_note-497] The number was similar around 2009, representing a slight numerical preponderance on the continent.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERezende,_Brasil:_ator_unipolar_na_América_do_Sul?_(2016)287-288-498]

In terms of technology, modern missile launchers coexist with [howitzers](14392.md) manufactured in the 1950s, with an average range of ten to fifteen kilometers. The most sophisticated systems are in the missile and rocket artillery, with [ASTROS II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astros_II) platforms organized into two Missile and Rocket Groups, totaling 78 launch, command post, communications, radars, workshops and meteorological vehicles. They are headquartered in [Formosa, Goiás](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formosa,_Goi%C3%A1s), and subordinate to the Army Artillery Command. This is the army's strategic artillery, with the greatest ranges — up to 90 kilometers with [cluster munition](79745.md) rockets or 300 kilometers with [cruise missiles](6590.md),[^cite_note-artilharia3-495][^cite_note-499] although the latter were not yet in service in 2023.[^cite_note-guiana-203]

Self-propelled field artillery units, which support the armored brigades,[^cite_note-500] have several models of the 155mm [M109 howitzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M109_howitzer), supported by [M992 A2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M992_field_artillery_ammunition_supply_vehicle) resupply vehicles. The rest of the field artillery uses 105 mm howitzers such as the [M101](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M101_howitzer) and [M102](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M102_howitzer), and [M114](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M114_155_mm_howitzer) 155 mm models. Paratroopers and light infantry are supported by [Oto Melara M56](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OTO_Melara_Mod_56) and [L118](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L118_light_gun) airborne howitzers, both 105 mm. The [120 mm M2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/120mm_M2_raiado) heavy rifled mortar is also important.[^cite_note-artilharia3-495]

Anti-aircraft artillery forces have 34 [Gepard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flakpanzer_Gepard) armored vehicles, distributed among the armored brigades, and [9K338 Igla-S](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9K38_Igla), [RBS 70](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RBS_70) and RBS NG portable surface-to-air missiles ([MANPADS](1389714.md)). All are of low altitude and range. The Brazilian Army does not have a medium-altitude anti-aircraft defense system.[^cite_note-501][^cite_note-502]

### Aviation

Main article: [Brazilian Army Aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Army_Aviation)



Army Aviation Helicopter

In 2022, Brazilian Army Aviation operated 95 helicopters of the [HA-1 Esquilo](2691322.md), [HM-1 Pantera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurocopter_AS565_Panther), [HM-2 Black Hawk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikorsky_UH-60_Black_Hawk), [HM-3 Cougar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurocopter_AS532_Cougar) and [HM-4 Jaguar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurocopter_EC725) models.[^cite_note-503] In the early 2000s, the Brazilian fleet was the second largest among South American armies. In line with most army aviation forces in the region, it focuses on transport helicopters and has no dedicated [attack helicopters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack_helicopter).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDullius,_Gastos_militares_na_América_do_Sul_(2008)15-504] The transport capacity of the aircraft based at the Taubaté Aviation Base in 2020, close to the 12th Light Infantry Brigade (Airmobile), is not enough to transport an entire infantry battalion in one wave.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouza,_A_importância_da_ampliação_da_força_de_helicópteros_no_assalto_aeromóvel_(2021)-505]
